Track K code (SPEC 14, Build phase). Written 2026-09-27. Nothing here was trained, queued or committed; no language
model was loaded. Tests: tiny CPU models only. No harness file was changed (K4 needs none: harness/budget.py already
solves an MLP width at a fixed total through attn_mult, fixed_d, fixed_layers and fit_mlp).

MODULES
  kcommon.py   SPEC constants, tagged seeds (default_rng([W = 7, tag])), sha256 and jsonl helpers, tokenizer check
  pools.py     SPEC 1 pools by rule (CAP 714 and LOW 3,656 left after the drops), K-EVAL-S triples (11,056 in
               1,382 cube cells since K3 round 2; 11,059 loose triples before)
  world.py     SPEC 2-3 fact base (N_CAP drawn once; FB_low and FB_high are prefixes), filler, slot plans, bio docs
  lookup.py    SPEC 6 hit and miss lines, K5 chats and K5 layout
  items.py     SPEC 4 item structure, rendering to id-carrying chat records, the generator-side world rule
  fams.py      families R, U, B, F, A, P;  skill.py  names per split, exact per-block flags, OOD columns, B eval
               cube groups (K3 round 3; reversed twins before); K3 round 4: earlier questions ask bystanders only,
               R and U reserve one; U's foil rank, R's, F's, P's (k, rank) and A's context kind block flags; P a
               foil in every item and a bystander entity (fams.fam_R by); K3 round 5: fam_R picks the key among
               symmetric holders by the follow flag, keeping orders on the holders' rank tuple (holder_ranks), fam_B
               asks the entity at the flagged rank of a uniform order; K3 round 6: fam_R, fam_U one law for every
               key, the asked key a uniform draw over the item's keys, sym_order and sigma_with (intro ranks apart
               from the structure), U's pairs (U_TYPES, U_Q), P's bystander a component of its own, fam_P passes
               None on; skill: no R, P or U follow flag, R's k 2-4, A_CTX (R 1/4), component() and the component
               rule for earlier questions (R, U, P, A both-items), P's question places (_questions place: no P
               item redrawn for its question count); K3 round 7: fam_A's both-item draw (U contexts: an entity
               uniform among the context's k entities, the context redrawn, context and all, while it lacks
               nothing; R contexts round 6's), A_CTX R 3/8 [wording corrected K3 round 8, REVIEW 9b D-1]; K3 round
               8: fam_F's ellipsis items, the key's and the foil's antecedents of one attribute (BD-48); G-2:
               skill._pad redraws a DFAR pad that would join an earlier question's entity to the final key's component
  oracles.py   SPEC 5 token-level parser, IDEAL and every shortcut oracle (O1-O10, O3_S, O5n, T, O9|10; R-1: O11-O14
               and the SURF, SURFe, SURFa pattern views; K3 round 2: B-CENTER, B-TOKFREQ, B-MAJOR, F-INTRO, F-DEFX,
               A1H; K3 round 3: B-ADAPT, B-FRAME, reported A-MANYFREE, A-QNEW, A-QREPEAT, F-ANTE; K3 round 5:
               R-POST, R's design posterior, and B-SURPRISE; K3 round 6: TA-SHARE, TA-REP, TA-CNT, TA-SHREP, and
               T's and their bar on R, U, F, P; K3 round 7: U-TIGHTSPAN, reported F-TURNK, tie answers, credit;
               K3 round 8: F-ANTESIZE (bar 0.65 on F) and reported A-CNTJOIN)
  purity.py    SPEC 5 acceptance (oracle thresholds with C5 tolerance, balance rules, purity, round trip; K3 round 4:
               early_name, all_free_asked, U's foil rank and R's, F's, P's rank per block, the B group count; K3
               round 5: exch_stats, R's, P's and B's exchangeability rules; K3 round 6: uniform places for R, U, P,
               exch_attr (the asked key among the asked entity's keys), exch_intro (every entity's intro rank
               against its size), early_comp, U's count cells, F's follow only; K3 round 7: A.exch_side; K3 round
               8: ante_same, F's per-item antecedent rule; G-2: item_fails, SPEC 4's per-item rules, read on the OOD
               sets too)
  evalsets.py  SPEC 8 files;  check_k.py  the acceptance over a whole data root, fact-stream purity included (K3
               round 4: a K-EVAL B set must score 3n/16 groups; round 7: ceilings(), SPEC 9's ceiling per family;
               round 15: check.json's code_sha256, the sha256 of every module the check runs, CHECK_CODE)
  gen.py       CLI writing pretokenize-format shards (uint16 .bin for TokenShardSource, id chat .jsonl for
               ChatJsonlSource), manifests with sha256s, the run config data: block per arm
  probes.py    SPEC 9 scorer (LIK with tie credit, CE_C, strict GEN, P marker, latent and expressed bits)
  ablate.py    K2/K3 hooks: unit, head and sublayer ablation (zero or mean), collect_means, prune_mlp (units
               removed, still trainable), reset_units (K3), trace (M4), unit_effects (M3), grad_x_act
  masks.py     K2 M1 HardConcrete masks (KF, SF, KS, SS; fit also returns p_drop, each unit's final P(closed);
               penalty, per budget unit since K3 round 1; K3 round 3: the better of 2 restarts by the exact
               objective, no warmup: a penalty warmup was tried and dropped), random_matched (M2, K3 P-R with
               skill-effect strata)
  cues.py      K3 round 1: the fitted cue model's non-resolution features per candidate (item_features; round 2:
               NAMES columns, and f_item_features, the F model's closed column list; round 3: its alias windows;
               round 4: _q_cols, the question-to-question columns, 164 pooled columns; round 6: ta_item_features,
               the attribute-blind model's 89 columns, candidates the asked entity's keys; round 7: its none mode,
               " none" a candidate with the asked name's side, _ta_side; round 8: f_item_features pv_cnt, pv_hold
               (the attribute of the statement just before a candidate: its statements and holders) and q_nattr (the
               asked name's other attributes in named statements), 124 F-model columns) [K3 round 9, REVIEW 10b D-2];
               G-2: the TA column osize live (0 on every item until G-2, REVIEW 12b D-1)
  cuegate.py   K3 round 1: its learners (logit, mlp, set), 2-fold cross-fit, bars; run() is gen.py check's gate
               (round 2: the pooled model, then the F model on F items; draw(), per-set tolerance; round 3: B cube
               groups for twin pairs; round 5: family-alone models, FAM_CAP 40,960 items each, R U B F P; round 6:
               ta_run, the attribute-blind model per family R U F P with T's bar, fails "(TA)"; round 7: ta_a_run,
               A's attribute-blind side, " none" against R, U, P keys at the stream's shares)
  k4_shapes.py K4 shapes via budget.solve; wrote ../K4_allocation/configs/model_H{4,6,7}.yaml
  ktrain.py, kprobe.py, kroute.py, kopt.py, prune.py, analyze_k.py, count_k.py: SPEC 14's harness pieces built K-side
               (the k: block of a run config), the K3 reset CLI, the analyzer core and the K10 count report (SPEC 14
               BUILD below; tests 187, tests/mutation_k14.py 53 mutants; 66 since REVIEW 14c's fixes; G-2:
               analyze_k.pick_g, the g pick at two selection seeds, 72 mutants; K3 round 14: 84; round 15: 97; round 16:
               107)
  kconfig.py   K3 round 16: the run-config writer for STAGE 0 and K1 (configs/k_base.yaml and every run config,
               written then checked against the registry, never overwritten; plans/k0.txt, k0x.txt, k1a.txt, k1b.txt;
               g_K read only from a final K0-g reading); configs/prereg.yaml (train.py's gate), plans/CURRENT;
               2026-11-22: k0.txt's grid waits for the smoke's pass mark (SMOKE_MARK)
  k0read.py    K3 round 16: K0-g's K-DEV objective per run and the pick (analyze_k.pick_g), appended to readings
  k0smoke.py   K3 round 16: K0-smoke's checks (hook against a probe-off replay, K-D1; shares, the FACT draw, memory,
               tok/s and hours); on a pass it writes the mark the K0-g grid waits for (2026-11-22)
  kpreflight.py  K3 round 16: E2's preflight --strict on a K config, its whole-list data rule replaced by K's (LANG
               sources, the data root's passed check by this code, the K-PROBE pin)
  queue_k.sh, launch_k.sh  K3 round 16: the K queue on E2's queue_lib.sh (ktrain.py entry, K scoring then bpb, gpu.lock
               per run; a K0-g run scores K-DEV alone, 2026-11-22) and its detached launcher (K's own bundle);
               tests/test_queue_k.py runs it with a fake python (tests/fake_kpy.py) and, without util-linux flock,
               tests/flock_shim.py; tests/mutation_k16.py holds round 16's build mutants (65); tests/test_k_cuda.py
               the CUDA kit (its cuda cases skip without CUDA; tests/mutation_kcuda.py, 4 mutants, is for the PC)
  tests/       276 tests, 4 of them CUDA-only and skipped on the Mac (K3 round 16: 52 new; 224 at K3 round 15, 7
               new; 217 at K3 round 14; 210 at G-2, 8 new; 202 at K3 round 13 and REVIEW
               14c's fixes; 187 at the SPEC 14 build, 155 at
               round 12, 153 at round 11, 150 at round 10, 147 at round 9, 146 at round 8, 141 at round 7, 133 at

               K3 round 7: 193,
               19 new, 5 retargeted; K3 round 6: 174, 27 new, 7
               retired with the round-5 R, U and A code they targeted, 8 retargeted; before: 154 =

COMMANDS ON THE PC (from experiments/K/code, planck_root linked at the code root as for E2; D below is the data root
the data: blocks point at)
  D=../../../planck_root/data/k
  python gen.py skill $D --workers 8
  for a in F0 FL FH CAP; do python gen.py fact $D --arm $a; done
  for a in L0 L50 L100; do python gen.py k5 $D --arm $a; done
  python gen.py evals $D
  python gen.py check $D          # exit 0 only if SPEC 5 acceptance passes (the K3 cue gate included; set
                                  # torch threads to 4 or fewer on a shared machine); report in $D/check.json
  python gen.py data $D --arm FH  # the data: block (also F0 FL CAP L0 L50 L100 CONT); LANG = E2's pinned shares
  python probes.py --ckpt RUN/final.pt --items $D/evals/k_eval_id.jsonl $D/evals/fb_probe.jsonl --out S.jsonl \
      --device cuda
  Measured on the Mac (load average about 77 from other work): gen.py fact --arm FH at full scale 45 s wall,
  204 MB of .bin and 225 MB of .jsonl, every FB entity 100 bio exposures; a 1/10-scale skill stream (163,840
  items, 12.3M tokens) 26 s with 8 workers and its check 48 s, pass. Full-scale skill, K5 and check times were
  not measured.

BUILD DEVIATIONS (numbered; each is a generator or check rule, never an item by hand; for the FIXED log)
  BD-1 B's statement order is uniform, so its follow rate is chance (1 - 1/k), not section 4's 1/2. Measured on one
       512 block with the 1/2 rule: B last-statement rate 0.348 vs chance 0.181, O3_12/13/23 0.72-0.73 (limit
       0.65 + 3 SE = 0.713). Uniform order: 0.162 vs 0.191, O3_S two-token subsets 0.56-0.58, eval twin pairs
       <= 0.20. Every O3_S foil is before the key in half the items by symmetry.
  BD-2 The last-statement rule passes at max(0.05, 3 binomial SE): eval families of 128-512 items made 0.05 about
       1.4-2 SE (C5's noise rule applied to this rule too).
  BD-3 Last-statement rate at chance in U and F: F always has one foil; U has a foil whenever k >= 2, stated 1..m
       times in update items and m times in two-slot controls; U's asked entity has 2-3 attributes (T on U was
       0.648 at n 41K, limit 0.657; now about 0.48). Measured U bias over 12 blocks: -0.005.
  BD-4 P's statements use R's design (foil and follow flags); SPEC 4 left P's value structure open.
  BD-5 U's 1/2 update rule is checked from tokens as "no-update controls exactly 1/4" (update and two-slot items
       look alike in tokens).
  BD-6 K5's FACT share is one chat source (K5 chats plus SPEC 3 fact-QA filler chats), no bio filler: at p = 1
       the K5 chats are 114.4M of the 123.6M FACT draw, and a separate bio source would leave no room to hold every
       K5 chat inside the drawn part of its own source. Filler tokens by arm: lookup.layout5.
  BD-7 Eval B items carry only the final question (a twin reversed the statements; since K3 round 3 a group's copies
       share them; an earlier question would change a gold). Training B items keep 1-3 questions.
  BD-8 K-EVAL-S B foils are K-EVAL-S triples too, so no trained name triple enters K-EVAL-S.
  BD-9 FB-PROBE, CONFLICT entity picks and K5 sets draw from seed 7201/7401/7501 streams; SPEC 8 names no FB-PROBE
       seed. Fact candidate sets have one size, round(mean |C|) of K-EVAL-ID, for every fact file (K2 notes say per
       split; the K2 splits use the same generator, so the sizes should agree, not checked).
  BD-10 The skill stream is written to at least 1.03 x its expected draw and overshoots by up to one pool round
       (8 rounds, about 6M tokens at full scale).
  BD-11 masks.fit defaults lr 0.05, Adam eps 1e-6 (the default 1e-8 turned float32 round-off gradients of about
       1e-9 into full steps on idle units); since K3 round 1 (R-5), 2,000 steps, lam 6 per budget unit (the penalty
       lam x relu(E[dropped] - b) / b), gate init 0.0 (R-2: 1,000 steps, lam 1 per unit; built: 300 steps, init
       1.0). K2 still fixes lam on seed 201's SPLIT A, from {4, 6, 10}, checked on the fixture (K2 notes K2-f).
  BD-12 F (R-1 redesign): orders whose last statement is the key's latest are kept at fams.LAST_KEEP = 0.5 (0.35 since
       K3 round 2, BD-21); without
       it F's last-statement rate read 0.261 against a chance 0.198, with it 0.201 against 0.199 (4 fresh training
       blocks, 2,048 items each way, final design; SPEC 4's limit is +-0.05). Every F item's order
       also puts each alias after its definition by a unit swap (fams._defs_first) before the order is checked.
  BD-13 SURF, SURFe, SURFa (purity.surf): per pattern the gold index most often right among fitted items, ties share
       the credit, an unseen pattern takes the latest statement; the training stream is 2-fold cross-fitted (even
       items fit the odd ones and back: leave-one-out reads low on exactly balanced patterns, in-sample high), and
       every eval set is scored with the training stream's fit (report["surf_counts"], per family and view).
  BD-14 F's key and foil state the asked attribute in one reference kind (2026-09-27, second pass on R-1). Flags:
       follow exact, the kind nested in it with the tie order flipped between the follow groups, so kind 1/2, follow
       1/2 and follow-with-alias 1/4 are all exact even in the 2-item K-DEV and K-PROBE F blocks.
  BD-15 (K3 R-4) No earlier question asks the final question's attribute, except U's lure (the final key), asked
       only where a later statement of that key follows (skill._questions); P redraws an item left with fewer than
       2 questions (PLONG 5). Checked on every item (purity: early_attr).
  BD-16 (K3 R-3) F keeps orders ending with the foil's reference statement at LAST_KEEP too; checked over a set
       (purity: F.ref_last, the key's and the foil's counts within 3 SE).
  BD-17 (K3 gate) U's key and foil are exchangeable (SPEC 4 U), k 2-4, and U's (k, intro rank) are exact block
       flags (drawn per item, the fixed K-EVAL-ID draw failed SPEC 5's rank rule at 3.0 SE in one cell).
  BD-18 (K3 gate) A's both-items are R or U contexts (1/2 each) asking an entity without the asked attribute; the
       context keeps its source key's d range (skill._in_range).
  BD-19 (K3 gate) gen.py check fits the cue model on a seeded draw of 81,920 training items (cuegate.CAP, the size
       measured here); the oracle, balance and purity checks still read every kept item (SPEC 12).
  BD-20 (K3 R-5) masks.fit: per-budget penalty and the defaults of BD-11.
  BD-21 (K3 round 2, F-INTRO-DEFX) F: the key's and the foil's reference statements, an ellipsis with its antecedent,
       come after both entities' first explicit mention and definition (fams.fam_F ok()); LAST_KEEP 0.35 (0.5 read
       F.last 0.228 vs chance 0.192 once BD-21 pushed the references later; 0.35 reads 0.196 vs 0.192).
  BD-22 (K3 round 2, B-CENTER) B: names from one cube {a, a'} x {b, b'} x {c, c'} (skill.Gen.cube): all 8 in 3/4
       of items, 2-4 of them otherwise, the asked name uniform among them; K-EVAL-S is a seeded 10% of the cube
       cells that pair each S position's tokens (pools.build: 1,382 cells, 11,056 triples; a random 10% of triples
       held about 14 whole cubes, so K-EVAL-S B items could not be all held-out names, BD-8). B's (k, intro rank)
       exact per block (per item, test_gen's gen.py sample failed SPEC 5's rank rule at 3.2 and 3.0 SE in two cells).
  BD-23 (K3 round 2, the gate) cues.py NAMES columns; cuegate.run fits the F model on F items after the pooled
       model, F's ceiling the larger; B pairs at C5's tolerance for the set's own pair count; cuegate.draw.
  BD-24 (K3 round 2, REVIEW 3b T-1, D-1) purity: question counts on every set (1-3, P 2-4, PLONG 5-7, rules or not);
       U's no-update cell (1, 2, follow) and its mirror (2, 1, no follow) exactly 1/4 per block, every (key, foil,
       follow) cell as often as its mirror (3 SE), U's and B's k and intro rank exact per block.
  BD-25 (K3 round 3, REVIEW 4a S-2) skill._in_range: in an F item the key's and the foil's aliases of the asked
       attribute sit 1-10 exchanges after BOTH their definitions (each alias 1-10 after its own before). purity:
       every F item's attribute aliases in both windows (alias_win), and F.xnear (on reference items with two
       aliases, the alias nearer after the asked name's definition is the key's as often as the foil's, 3 SE).
  BD-26 (K3 round 3, REVIEW 4a S-1) B eval blocks (K-EVAL-ID, K-EVAL-S) replace reversed twins with cube groups
       (skill.group): of n items (a multiple of 32), 3n/16 cube items each followed by 3 copies asking the asked
       name's two-token neighbours (meta grp 0-3), n/4 items of 2-4 names; frames exact within the cube and the small
       items (block_flags ncube). purity and cuegate score a group as all 4 right (the product of the gate's credits),
       bar oracles.GROUP_TH 0.125; the per-block shares count the copies, the intro-rank rules do not. evalsets
       gives a B block with groups at least 32 items (gen.py evals --small: 32).
  BD-27 (K3 round 3, REVIEW 4b M-1; wording K3 round 4, REVIEW 5a D-1 and 5b D-a) masks.fit keeps the better of 2
       restarts (gate noise seeds s and s + 7919) by the exact objective, its weights included, with the hardened set
       ablated ("hard"); the penalty is lam x relu(E[dropped] - b) / b from step 0 (a linear warmup of lam over 500 or
       1,000 steps was tried and dropped: MX-KF single runs stuck in 13 of 104 with it against 2 of 200 without);
       tests/mask_sweep.py reads fit's own result for one step count (restarts included, per restart found or not)
       and needs --restarts 1 to read several step counts inside one run.
  BD-28 (K3 round 4, REVIEW 5a S-1) skill._questions: earlier questions ask bystanders' keys only (never the final
       entity or a holder of the final attribute; U's lure aside); make_item: R and U items reserve one bystander,
       drawn uniformly, that no question asks (A's both-items are R and U contexts whose asked entity is never
       asked); skill._pad: a DFAR pad states the asked attribute only for a holder. purity: early_name and
       all_free_asked on every item. B items (every entity a holder) hold one question.
  BD-29 (K3 round 4, REVIEW 5a S-2) U: the (key, foil) intro-rank pair is a block flag (skill.rank_pairs: each role's
       rank within one of n/k per k, every ordered pair within one of its share); purity k{k}_frank{j} per block.
       fam_A's U contexts draw k and the rank pair the same way (per item).
  BD-30 (K3 round 4) P: R's design plus a bystander entity (fams.fam_R by: the last slot, 1-3 attributes, never the
       asked one) and a foil in every item (follow 1/2): 3-5 entities, PLONG 3-5.
  BD-31 (K3 round 4) R's, F's and P's (k, intro rank) exact per block (skill.ranks; purity k{k}_rank{j} per block);
       OOD ENTS and PLONG keep their own k and a per-item rank (fl k_ood); A's both-item context kind (R or U) a
       block flag.
  BD-32 (K3 round 4, REVIEW 5b O-1) purity.group_count_fails: every B cube item (grp 0) opens a scored group;
       check_k: K-EVAL-ID's and K-EVAL-S's B sets score 3n/16 groups.
  BD-33 (K3 round 4, REVIEW 5a S-1; SPEC 5's ruling) cues._q_cols: the final name among earlier questions' names
       (times asked, the latest, distinct attributes asked of it), distinct names asked, the item's names stating
       nothing of the asked attribute that no question named; 149 -> 164 pooled columns.
  BD-34 (K3 round 5, REVIEW 6a S-1) fams.fam_R (R, P, A's R contexts): m holders (the key and the foil, plus each
       other entity, P's bystander aside, with probability R_HOLD 1/3; 1 in no-foil items), every entity 1-3
       attributes, a holder's with the asked one, a non-holder's without; j, the key's place among the holders in the
       order they state the attribute (the last if not follow, else uniform over the rest); t, the holders' intro
       ranks in that order, uniform over the injective tuples with t[j] the block's rank; orders of a structure, then
       structures, drawn until the holder ranks are t (R_TRIES 60 x 2,000, (m, j, t) kept), the holder at j relabelled
       slot 0. P(t) is the same under every hypothesis j, so the posterior over holders is the follow prior alone.
  BD-35 (K3 round 5, REVIEW 6a S-2) fams.fam_B: a uniform order (BD-1), the asked entity the one at the block's intro
       rank, relabelled slot 0 (every entity is a holder, so one always is there).
  BD-36 (K3 round 5) oracles R-POST (R and P, bar 0.65) and B-SURPRISE (BPART: O3_S's bars per item and on groups);
       purity.exch_stats: R.exch_rank, R.exch_size, P.exch_rank, P.exch_size, B.exch, Pearson chi-square over the
       cells with 5 or more expected, failing over purity.wh(df) (Wilson-Hilferty, 3.7 SD, a tail of about 1e-4).
  BD-37 (K3 round 5, REVIEW 6a G-1) cuegate.run: each of R, U, B, F, P alone on up to FAM_CAP 40,960 of its own
       training items (a seeded draw per family, salt 880 + its place in FAM_MODELS), the same columns and learners;
       a family's ceiling is the largest of its pooled, alone and (F) F models'; fails tagged "(F alone)" etc.
  BD-38 (K3 round 6, REVIEW 7a S-1; supersedes BD-34) fams.fam_R (R, P, A's R contexts): the main component's km
       entities (R 2-4; P 2-4 plus its bystander) each state 2-3 of R_Q 3 attributes (a uniform subset, each key
       once); the asked key a uniform draw over all keys (edges); the order sym_order(units, sigma_with(k, {e0:
       rank})): a uniform intro order with the asked entity at the block's rank, each entity introduced by one of its
       units drawn uniformly, every other unit at a uniform gap after its entity's intro unit. by (P): one more
       entity stating 1-3 attributes from the 3 the main component does not use. No rejection loop (R_TRIES gone).
  BD-39 (K3 round 6, REVIEW 7a S-1) fams.fam_U: every attribute a pair of entities (uniform over the pairs, redrawn
       until every entity is in one; U_Q: k 2 -> 2-3 attributes, 3 -> 3-4, 4 -> 4) with a count pair from U_TYPES
       ((1, 2) 1/2, two of 2-4 apart 1/4, (m, m) m 2-3 1/4; sides at random); attribute 0's type and side from the
       block (noupd (1, 2), upd one (2, 1), sym, twoslot); the order sym_order with the key's and the foil's intro
       ranks the block's (rank, frank; a stale frank, as in a planted test with k and rank per item, is redrawn).
       skill.block_flags: no follow flag for R, U, P, no foil flag; R's k 2-4 (P 3-5) in thirds, rank exact.
  BD-40 (K3 round 6, REVIEW 7a "+eq") skill._questions comp (R, U, P, A both-items): earlier questions name no
       entity of the component (skill.component: entities joined by a shared attribute) of the final entity and the
       holders; purity.item_facts early_comp, a fail on every set. kfix.old_questions takes comp.

     of it (REVIEW 3a); the smallest name set where every name's every proper subset is held by another name is the
     cube {a, a'} x {b, b'} x {c, c'} (each name needs a neighbour along each position, so each 2-position projection
     has rows and columns of 2 or more: at least 4 cells, 8 names), and in the cube every name is equally central.
     B's struct items (3/4) name the whole cube, the rest 2-4 of it (k 2, 3, 4 in thirds), the asked name uniform
     among them, (k, intro rank) exact per block. K-EVAL-S B items need whole held-out cubes, and a random 10% of
     triples holds about 14 of them, so K-EVAL-S is now a seeded 10% of the cube cells that pair each S position's

  S-1 RULING (SPEC 5, on its LINK paragraph's principle). The skill R, U, B and A test is joining the final
     question's name to the statements that carry it. LINK makes that join through full-name matches (the final
     question to an earlier one, its answer's statement, that statement's name), so it is the skill and no column.
     REVIEW 5a's QFIT and QSIZE make no join: question to question (the final name among earlier questions' names)
     and statement to statement (holders' sizes) never meet at a name, only through a count the generator correlated.
     Both classes are cheap (same_q_before, A-QREPEAT and A-QNEW already used the first; n_attrs, ent_named and
     own_cnt the second), so the cue is cheap: the gate carries its columns and the generator must not feed it. No
     column compares a candidate's name with any question's, the final one's or an earlier one's (with question-to-
     question identity that is LINK's join); ans_same_q (the copy cue) stays, a join carried on purpose (it can only
     raise the ceiling, and SPEC 4 makes its value stale). SPEC 5's LINK paragraph, cues.py's docstring and the new
     "Ruling on name identity" paragraph say so.
     Columns (BD-33, cues._q_cols; 149 -> 164 pooled): qn_asked 0/1/2+, qn_last, qn_attrs 0/1/2+ (the final name among
     earlier questions' names), q_names 0-3+ (distinct names asked), q_free_unasked 0-3+ (names stating nothing of the
     asked attribute that no question named). Power: the round-4 gate on REVIEW 5a's round-3 fresh sets (train12573,
     eval12574, evals12575; k3/round4/gate_old.log): R 0.695 / 0.709 / 0.706, A 0.662 / 0.690 / 0.686, P 0.776 /
     0.772 / 0.781, 9 fails (U 0.576-0.608, B 0.195-0.276, F 0.625-0.636, F model 0.626-0.629 pass); round 3's gate
     read 0.642-0.654 there (REVIEW 5a's base run).

  S-1 R-BAYES (bayes_r.py). fams.fam_R keeps a uniform order among those in which the key has the block's intro rank
     AND the block's follow status (arrange + _rank_follow), so under "holder h is the key" the observed order has
     probability 1 / (n! frac(h)), frac(h) the share of random orders of the same statements giving h its observed
     rank and follow status. R-BAYES scores each holder by prior(follow: 5/8 or 3/8) x (1 / n_h) x sum over the other
     holders f of 1 / n_f (the key and the foil draw 1-3 attributes with the asked one forced, other names 1-3 of
     all 6) / frac(h) (Monte Carlo, 4,000 orders) and the d range. Reads only the named statements' order grouped by
     name, the names' intro order, which statements carry the attribute and each holder's exchange distance; the
     question's name never. 0.6717 / 0.6609 / 0.6797 / 0.6777 / 0.6602, stream 0.6721 / 0.6788 (whole stream
     0.673); tag 16676 0.6734. Over C5 on fresh train (limit 0.664), both stream shards (0.661, 0.672), the whole
     stream (0.660) and tag 16676 (0.657: 9.9 SE over 0.65); under the smaller eval sets' limits (0.682-0.739).
     Ablation (bayes_abl.py; tag 16676 / fresh train / stream shards): the follow prior alone 0.619 / 0.619 / 0.616 /
     0.627; with sizes 0.623-0.636; with frac over the rank alone 0.625-0.637; over the follow status alone
     0.630-0.644; the joint frac 0.671-0.679; the d range adds 0.000; frac over the rank with nothing else
     0.585-0.599. So the cue is the joint condition: rank and follow are drawn independently per block, while in
     random orders an early-introduced holder tends to state early and be followed, and the key carries the pairs
     random orders rarely make. SPEC 16's "R sits at its bar by design" understates it: the design's cheap ceiling
     is about 0.673, and K1 reads R's LIK against its ceiling.
     Fitted, R items only (run6r.py; cues.py's 164 columns, cuegate's learners unchanged, fitted on tag 16676's
     40,960 R items; cross-fit / fresh train / stream (20,480)): mlp 0.651 / 0.667 / 0.664, set 0.644 / 0.663 /
     0.667, att6 0.659 / 0.671 / 0.667 (its cross-fit over 0.657 too); the four small eval sets 0.646-0.684, under
     their limits. With feats6's columns: mlp 0.650 / 0.666 / 0.665, att6 0.651 / 0.668 / 0.665, gbdt6 0.648 / 0.659
     / 0.664. The same mlp and set fitted on 10,240 R items read 0.643 / 0.645 and 0.641 / 0.642 (tag 16676, stream),
     0.631 / 0.634 cross-fitted on 5,120: the gate's columns carry the cue, its training size hides it (G-1).
  S-2 B-SURPRISE (bayes_b2.py; B-ADAPT's proper subset of the question's name positions, O3_S's class, the tie

  FINDINGS
     S-1 R over its bar with a question-blind cheap rule: R-BAYES 0.671-0.679 on every large set (fresh train,
       stream shards, 40,960 fresh R items; limits 0.657-0.672); cues.py's columns with cuegate's mlp and set read
       0.663-0.667 (att6 0.667-0.671) when fitted on 40,960 R items. Cause: fams.fam_R conditions the order on the
       key's rank and follow flags jointly (S-1 ablation). Fix direction: condition the order on nothing
       key-specific that random orders tie to the key's statements (proto3 0.573, proto4 0.606), then decide SPEC
       4's R follow share (0.44-0.45 natural) and rank exactness; choosing the key between key and foil after the
       order (proto) is not enough (0.663).
     S-2 B per item on training items over O3_S's bar with a partial key and a question-blind tie-break (B-SURPRISE
       0.676-0.681, B-BAYESTIE 0.677-0.688); eval sets and groups hold. Fix prototyped: ask the name at the flagged
       rank of a free order (0.621 / 0.626, purity passes).
     G-1 the gate's ceiling is a finite-sample estimate that missed S-1 (above).
     Checked and within their bars: F (9 rules, two DIAGs, F models 0.594-0.668, pooled 0.619-0.660), U, P
       (P-BAYES, which carries P's rank normalisation, 0.551-0.612), A (A1H's class only), B groups. No rank-flag
       effect over a bar elsewhere: F's is symmetric (diag_f6r.py), U's key and foil ranks are flags together.
  VERDICT: not clean (S-1: R over its bar with R-BAYES on every large set; S-2: B's per-item O3_S bar on training
     items). F, U, P, A, B groups and the gate code hold.

  S-1 (R; BD-34, SPEC 4 R [K3r5]). Reproduced on fresh build-design blocks (sim_r.py, tag 51001, 4,096 R items):
     R-BAYES 0.681. Design: m holders (the key, the foil, each other entity with probability R_HOLD 1/3, P's bystander
     aside; 1 in no-foil items), every entity 1-3 attributes, the asked one among a holder's; j, the key's place among
     the holders in the order they state the attribute, is the last when not follow and uniform over the rest when
     follow; t, the holders' intro ranks in that order, uniform over the injective tuples with t[j] the block's rank;
     orders of a structure, then structures, drawn until the holder ranks are t, (m, j, t) kept; the holder at j is
     relabelled slot 0. P(C, key at j) = P(f(j)) P(j | f(j), m) (1/k) P(t | t_j) Q(C | t), every factor but the first
     two the same for every j given C (t is read off C), so the posterior over holders is the follow prior alone; R's
     rank and follow stay exact per block. Design ceiling R-POST 0.2 + 0.8 (19/27 x 5/8 + 8/27 x 3/8) = 0.641 (O2 <=
     0.50 with the no-foil fifth forces follow in 5/8 of foil items; 3 or more holders: the last at 3/8 beats each
     followed one at 5/16 or less); P 0.50 (follow 1/2).

  FINDINGS
     S-1 (R, U, P: design; the gate covers only the name-blind side) An attribute-blind topic tracker, T's class,
       barred at 0.65 on R, U, F since C4, beats that bar by choosing the asked key among the asked entity's keys
       from question-blind attribute statistics: R TA-SHARE 0.734-0.761 (over C5 on 5 of 6 sets), fitted
       0.746-0.818 (every set); U TA-CNT 0.688-0.750 (5 of 6), fitted 0.871-0.940 (every set); P TA-SHARE
       0.705-0.724 (4 of 6), fitted mlp 0.750-0.792 (every set). Cause: SPEC 4 builds the asked key unlike the
       entity's other keys. R and P give its attribute 2 or more holders in every foil item (R 80%, P all) while the
       entity's other attributes get the random draw's holders, mostly fewer; U restates the asked key 2-4 times
       (upd, twoslot) and gives it a foil holder while the entity's other keys are stated 1-2 times. C4's "T near
       0.61 if 1-3 attributes are uniform" assumed the asked attribute is uniform among the entity's attributes to a
       reader blind to the question's attribute; it is not. The gate cannot see this: its columns are blind to the
       question's name, and the attribute-blind side is carried by T and O5n alone. So R's, U's and P's LIK over 0.65
       would not show attribute binding (R and U are half of S_ID). Fix direction: build the asked entity's keys by
       one law (holder counts and repeat counts drawn the same for every key, the asked one then chosen uniformly
       among them, every later condition read the same for every key), and carry the attribute-blind side in SPEC 5
       with T's bar: TA-SHARE and TA-CNT as oracles and an attribute-blind fitted model (ta_fit.py's columns).
     S-2 (P: SPEC 5's threshold list) T reads 0.669 / 0.671 / 0.676 / 0.676 / 0.660 / 0.666 on P, over C5 on the
       fresh 40,960 (limit 0.657) and the stream (0.665); round 5 logged 0.666-0.676 "(no bar on P)". P's bar is
       "T's and O5n's bar" (SPEC 5), P's answers are R's recall, and the gate cannot carry T (it reads the question's
       name): T's 0.65 belongs on P too.
     Checked and within their bars: F (9 name-blind rule variants, the F model with 16 new columns, 4 attribute-blind
       rules and fit), B (4 tie-breaks, per item and on groups), R, U, P name-blind (11 rules, 3 fits with 11 new
       columns), A (3 A1H-class rules, each a mix loss; A-TA reported), the gate code.
  VERDICT: not clean (S-1: the attribute-blind class beats T's bar on R and U and P's 0.65 bar, as rules and fitted;
     S-2: T on P over P's bar). The name-blind side of every family, F, B, A and the gate code hold.

     name as T does and never its attribute picked the asked key. Change: one law for every key and a uniform draw of
     the asked key over the item's keys. R (fams.fam_R): 2-4 entities (k exact per block), each states 2-3 of R_Q 3
     attributes (a uniform subset, each key once), the asked key uniform over all keys (edges), so given the item it

  FINDINGS
     S-1 (A; design and gate) An attribute-blind A detector beats A's bar without the mix loss SPEC 5 gives as the
       reason A1H's class is reported, not barred. A-ONEKEY3 reads A 0.562-0.572 on every set (over C5 on the four
       large ones and K-EVAL-ID) and gains in the mix on 5 of 6 (+0.0007 to +0.0014; K-EVAL-ID's 512 per family
       -0.0021). It reads the asked name's statements as T does, the item's names and its two-holders-per-attribute
       structure (the same whichever key is asked) and question-to-question identity; never the asked attribute's
       statements or the question's attribute token. Cause (a_mech8.py, reads meta): an A both-item from a U context
       (3/4 of both-items since round 6's A_CTX) asks a non-holder drawn uniformly, while a U item asks an entity
       drawn by its size (a uniform key); at k 3 the asked name states one attribute in 1,166 of 3,086 such A items
       (0.378) against 1,088 of 13,680 U items (0.080) (k 4: 0.464 against 0.153), and A's U contexts have k 3 or 4
       only. At A_CTX 1/2 the same fresh-train cell counts give a mix change of -0.0011 (arithmetic, not run). The
       gate cannot see it: the pooled columns never count the asked name's attributes and the TA model skips A. Fix
       direction: draw an A both-item's asked entity so its T-side statistics (attribute count, statement counts,
       partners' counts, intro rank, k) follow the U and R items' asked entity's law, checked with A-ONEKEY3 and
       A-TCELL; and carry A's attribute-blind side in the gate (ta_item_features with a " none" row on A against R, U
       and P at the stream's shares, or A1H-class oracles barred when they do not lose in the mix).
     G-1 (U; the gate's ceiling, not a bar) U's cheap ceiling is about 0.60, not the gate's 0.555: U-BAYES and the
       simpler U-TIGHTSPAN read 0.585-0.607 on the large sets and 0.602 on gen.py's stream, whose gate cross-fit reads
       0.555 on the same 23,040 records (K-EVAL-ID 0.594 / 0.595 against 0.549; K-EVAL-S 0.652 / 0.650 against 0.648,
       lure share 18%). Under 0.65 and within C5 everywhere. The pooled columns lack the key's span and the earlier
       questions inside it (with them the reviewer's model reads 0.5926-0.6445). Round 6's open item: non-lure U items
       read 0.520-0.545 by U-BAYES against 0.50, lure items 1.000. SPEC 9 reads LIK against the ceiling, so U's should
       be the larger of the gate's and U-TIGHTSPAN's (or the gate given those columns).
     N-1 (F; text) The class SPEC 5 keeps out of the F model (names in an ellipsis's turn) now reads over F's bar
       (F-TURNK 0.669-0.684, F-FOILTURN 0.643-0.666; REVIEW 3a's F-TURNN read 0.568-0.593 on round 1's F), while
       every counted rule reads 0.604-0.646 and the 93-column model 0.623-0.641. F holds by that exclusion, which SPEC
       5 states; its size (an ellipsis link: alias halves 0.48-0.53) belongs beside F's ceiling for a K1 reader.
     Checked and within their bars: F (6 rules both ways, the 93-column model on 81,920 items), R and P (5 rules
       each, NB and TA models at the design values), U's TA side (0.5435-0.5589), B (5 tie-breaks, B-GBDT, groups),
       the gate code (GOLD, ATTR, NAME, FOLDS).
  VERDICT: not clean (S-1: an A1H-class rule over A's bar that gains in the mix, which the gate cannot see). G-1 is
     a ceiling understatement under the bar; N-1 is text.

  FINDINGS
     G-1 (F; SPEC 9's ceiling, under the bar) A rule with no fit reads F over the ceiling SPEC 9 prints for it.
       F9-ANTESIZE reads 0.6360 on 81,920 fresh items, where the repo's F readers fitted on the same tags read
       0.626-0.628 (fgate9.py), and 0.6379 on gen.py's stream against its F ceiling 0.631 (SURF; gate 0.6305); the
       fcand9 model reads 0.6400 fresh and 0.6440 on the stream (K-EVAL-ID 0.6582 against 0.643, within noise there).
       Every reading is under C5, so F's bar holds. The rule is the count shadow of F-ANTE (reported, 0.69-0.72): it
       reads the attribute of the statement before each ellipsis (its frequency, never its name) and how many
       attributes the asked name states, and gains because the key never restates its own antecedent's attribute
       while the foil's is one the key states in 0.20-0.40 of items (fams._ref_units: each entity states each other
       attribute once). The F model's closed list (SPEC 5) has neither column, so the gate cannot see it, and SPEC 5
       does not say whether an antecedent's attribute, read without its name, is part of the resolution. Fix hint:
       rule on that class. If cheap: carry F9-ANTESIZE as an F ceiling row (as U-TIGHTSPAN for U) or give the F model
       the attribute frequency of the statement before a reference and the asked name's attribute count; a design
       cure makes the antecedent attribute's frequency independent of whose ellipsis it is (for example both
       antecedents stating an attribute both entities hold). If not cheap: name it with F-ANTE in SPEC 5 and print it
       beside F's ceiling as F-TURNK is. Until then a K1 F LIK between 0.631 and about 0.645 is not above every reader
       that matches no name to a reference.
     O-1 (A; a ruling, text) A reader of both sides, comparing them through counts, beats A's bar with a mix gain. The
       one-sided readers hold (ta_a_run's columns 0.500-0.5035 under logit, mlp and the GBDT; the pooled columns
       0.501-0.508 under the GBDT), but ta_a_run's columns plus the asked attribute's side as counts on the " none" row
       read A 0.668-0.719 (" none" on 0.34-0.44 of both-items, 0.009-0.033 of R, U, P; a mix gain of about +0.014; the
       statement count alone carries it), and the rule A9-CNTJOIN reads A 0.732-0.766 with a mix gain of +0.023 to
       +0.027 while never firing on R, U or P. It joins the question's attribute to the asked name's keys through a
       statistic instead of the token: A's binding, done lossily. SPEC 5's cheap classes are one-sided (blind to the
       name, or to the attribute) plus question-to-question and statement-to-statement comparisons, and REVIEW 5a's
       ruling covers names only, so nothing says where this reader belongs. Fix hint: one sentence in SPEC 5 beside the
       ruling on name identity: a reader that compares the question's attribute with the asked name's keys, by token or
       by a count (statements, holders), does A's binding, so it is skill, not a cheap reader; K1 can then split A's LIK
       by A9-CNTJOIN's cell (it fires on about half of A's both-items) to show whether a gain over 0.50 needs token
       binding. If A must measure token binding only, A's both-items would need the asked attribute's statement and
       holder counts drawn equal to one of the asked name's keys' (a design change, not proposed here).
     Checked and within their bars: F (7 new rules both ways, 0.6055-0.6445; two fitted models on 81,920 items); R and P
       (8 rules each, NB and TA GBDT models at the design values); U (8 rules and 4 copy variants, NB 0.587-0.652, TA
       0.543-0.578); B (4 tie-breaks, a GBDT, every group 0.000 or under 0.052); A (3 one-sided rules, each a mix loss;
       the one-sided fitted readers at 0.50); purity.accept on four fresh sets; the gate code (GOLD, ATTR, FOLDS).

SPEC 14 BUILD 2026-10-11 (Track K workflow W3: SPEC 14's pieces NOT BUILT HERE lists, the K10 count report and the
  compile decision; research/CUTS_AUDIT_2026-10.md rows 134, 135 and 25. Mac CPU only: no language model loaded, tiny
  CPU test models, nothing trained, queued or committed; no package installed; no harness file and no other
  workflow's path changed. Scripts and outputs: the session scratchpad, k3/s14/)
  WHY K-SIDE. Today's ownership puts harness/ with another workflow, so every harness piece SPEC 14 names is built
    here as a wrapper of harness/train.py: ktrain.py runs train.main with train.build_loader, train.make_optimizer
    and train.Trainer wrapped for that one call (restored after it; a test checks). The keys sit in a top-level k:
    block, which harness train.py ignores: SPEC 14's eval.k_probe is k.probe, train.grad_route is k.grad_route, the
    per-group weight-decay multiplier is k.wd_mult and the weights-only start is k.init_weights. Run by harness
    train.py, a config with a k: block would drop it without a word, so every K run starts through ktrain.py, which
    writes out_dir/k_start.jsonl (the k block, its sha256, the config's sha256, what it installed) and puts k_sha256
    in every checkpoint; the run-reading step must refuse a K run without them.
  BUILT (new files only; the 44 files of k3/rev14b/before.sha hash as before but this file)
    ktrain.py  the entry; refuses an unknown k key, train.compile other than false or unset when a k: block is set
               (K COMPILE DECISION), grad_route outside pack mode or naming an unknown source, and init_weights
               with schedule.init_from. With no k: block it calls train.main untouched.
    kprobe.py  the K-PROBE hook (SPEC 8, 9): every N steps and at the last, probes.score on the plain model (fp32,
               doc=None, right padding; mode and device untouched); the torch RNG states are compared around each
               reading (a change raises); errors are logged and training goes on. out_dir/k_probe.jsonl: probes'
               summary per set and family, skill_lik and fact_acc per set. Files are sha256-pinned; sets and take
               pick K5's 256 FB-MISS and 256 FB-HIT (K5 notes EXTRA 2).
    kroute.py  grad_route (K3-R): RoutedLoader (the harness Loader plus each position's source; every window is
               packed again as a shadow of token indices with the window's own rng and checked token by token, ids,
               doc and pos, against the real rows), RoutedSwiGLU (same parameters and state_dict; the forward is the
               plain F.linear bit for bit; a custom backward with allowed[t, j] = fact[t] == reserved[j] masks the
               activation's gradient and the W_down columns' gradient), RoutedForward (each training micro-batch's
               mask, in the order the trainer drew them). Eager only.
    kopt.py    wd_mult (K4b): the SwiGLU matrices move to a group "matrix_mlp" right after "matrix", weight decay
               times the multiplier, before the first step.
    prune.py   K3's reset on a checkpoint (ablate.reset_units; --units JSON, --block-last n for K3-R, or no reset for
               P-0 and F0-cont) -> a weights-only file {model, model_cfg, k_prune}; k.init_weights loads it (sha256
               pinned) with a fresh optimizer and a fresh loader at the config's seed or data_seed; a resume from the
               run's own out_dir wins.
    analyze_k.py  SPEC 10's KR (pooled SD_ref and df, thr, HIGHER, LOWER, EQUAL, UNRESOLVED), DIVERGENCE (a diverged
               arm seed scores the worst value, a diverged reference seed leaves the contrast), UNSTABLE, CE-DISAGREE,
               WORSE_bpb as GENERAL-LANGUAGE COST, the Holm reading (two-sided p on t(df), step-down at 0.05), SPEC 9's
               time course from k_probe.jsonl (peak of the 5-checkpoint moving mean, final = mean of the last 5,
               TRANSIENT on 2 of 3 seeds), summaries from scorer rows (S_ID, S_ID-S, per family, F per the key's
               reference kind, B cube vs 2-4 names and cube-group accuracy, OOD columns), the append-only readings
               file. t from E3's e3analyze (scipy is not in the venv): t(0.975) at df 4, 6, 8 = 2.776, 2.447, 2.306.
    count_k.py the K10 count report (below).
  TESTS (4 new test files and tests/mutation_k14.py, 32 tests; 187 in all) [count corrected, REVIEW 14c D7]
    test_ktrain.py (10): with no k block, with the probe, and with wd_mult 1, a 12-step CPU run equals harness
      train.py's bit for bit (weights, data state, torch RNG, token counts, every log record; the optimizer too, but
      for wd_mult's extra group); readings at steps 5, 10 and 12 equal probes.score on the final checkpoint through
      probes.model_fn; a reading that draws from the RNG stops the run; file pins, sets and take; the wd groups and
      values, and one optimizer step at x1 and x4 from equal gradients moves every non-MLP parameter identically and
      every MLP one further toward 0; the weights-only start (the step-0 checkpoint is the file, optimizer state
      empty, loader unread, then a resume); prune's unit checks; the refusals; nothing left patched after a run.
    test_kroute.py (5): the routed loader yields the harness loader's batches bit for bit over 40 batches, its tags
      match the content (chats open with a role id) through a resume; routed gradients equal an independent
      reference (linear(h A) + linear(h (1 - A)).detach()) over two micro-batches; FACT-only and other-only positions
      touch only their own block (W_gate and W_up rows and W_down columns exactly zero elsewhere) while attention
      learns from both; a misaligned shadow packing raises; a routed ktrain run starts from the plain run's weights
      with its first loss, then differs.
    test_analyze_k.py (12) and test_count_k.py (5): every KR branch on hand-computed fixtures, DIVERGENCE both ways,

  FINDINGS (each a survivor probed for what it lets through)
    T-1 medium r15_skill_unshuffled (gen.py write_skill: order = np.arange instead of the seeded permutation)
      survives all 210 tests. The permutation is the only thing that mixes families in the SKILL stream: harness
      ChatJsonlSource reads each shard's lines in file order (shuffle permutes shard order only) and gen.data_block
      sets shuffle False. On rev15b/g the longest run of one family is 8 records; unshuffled it would be whole
      rounds' blocks (2,560 R, 2,560 U, 1,536 B, ... per round of 10,240), so training would cycle family by family
      and SPEC 9's time course and TRANSIENT would read the cycle. Hint: a test_gen test that reads the written
      skill shards and bounds the family run length (or the family shares per window of 512 records).
    T-2 medium pick_g's table validation. (a) r15_pick_grid_point_missing_ok (the "every grid g present" check
      off) survives: no test hands pick_g a table missing a grid point, so a lost g-check run would let a pick from
      two points pass. (b) Not a mutant: pick_g accepts an extension the grid pick did not name. Run on this tree:
      grid pick 1.0 (interior) plus a 0.25 point at 2.9 -> g 0.25, final True; grid pick 2.0 (an edge, asking 4.0)
      plus a 0.25 point -> g 2.0, final True, extend None, though K1 notes K0-g allow one point beyond an edge pick
      and only that one. Hint: refuse an extra point unless it is the extension the grid alone names, with tests.
    T-3 medium-low SPEC 10's deltas are unpinned: r15_delta_sid (DELTA S_ID 0.03 -> 0.02) and r15_bits_delta_scale
      (bits_delta 10% -> 1% of the reference mean) survive; no test reads analyze_k.DELTA or bits_delta. No reading
      script uses them yet (NOT BUILT), but they are the analyzer core's constants. Hint: one test against SPEC 10's
      values (S_ID 0.03, GEN 0.05, rates 0.10, bits 10%).
    T-4 medium-low r15_route_empty_sources_ok (ktrain.check's "not gr['sources']" dropped) survives: a K3-R config
      with sources [] would run with no FACT position, the reserved block never trained. test_refusals has no
      empty-sources case. Hint: add ({"grad_route": {"sources": [], "reserved": 4}}, "non-empty").
    T-5 low r15_probe_missing_set_ok (kprobe's "holds no record of set" check off) survives because

      K5's two sets a misspelt one would drop silently (analyze_k.probe_curve would then KeyError on that set).

batch 2, whose harness/ must not reach main yet), as main plus experiments/K. Each fix has a test watched failing:
on the frozen code, or on the mutant of the fixed line (mutation_k16.py, mutation_k.py's runner):
  1  k0smoke.py's hook_off check compared every log field but loss, gnorm and tok_per_s, and runio.append_jsonl
     stamps every log.jsonl record with the wall clock ("time"), which differs across three runs made one after
     another: same_data, bitwise and repeatable were False on every real smoke, so no pass mark could be written and
     the K0-g grid could never start. It now compares K-D1's fields by name (D1_FIELDS: step, tokens, sup_tokens,
     lr_factor, phase; D1_PREFIXES: drawn_, dropped_), each record must carry the named five and a drawn_ count, and
     the bitwise pair drops CLOCK (time, tok_per_s). K-D1 as registered (SPEC K3r16 (a), K1 notes: "every data and
     schedule field") is unchanged. Tests: test_a_clean_smoke_in_the_harness_log_format_passes (the review's probe),
     test_hook_off_compares_each_k_d1_field (7 cases), test_a_log_without_a_named_field_fails_same_data (4 cases);
     mutants kr_smoke_* and rv_smoke_phase_ignored (the review's, ported to the new list).

     tree holds (k0; k0x at the one extension its configs hold; k1 from the tree's results/readings.jsonl) and
